Live on the iPhone 17 Simulator (iOS 26.5) through the real MCP server: `compare` before any screenshot is refused, after one it is `same` (32 bytes, 0.24 s), after `tap General` it is `changed: 1 region, 49.3% of the screen` with twelve lines and `and 14 more` (563 bytes), against 115,912 bytes for the picture. Live through the command line: Auto-Capitalisation switched off gives `changed: 1 region, 0.6% of the screen`, `region 304,592 64x40 (no text changed)`; switched on again gives `same`. I looked at both boxed pictures: one box around the whole changed page, one box exactly around the switch.

**Limits.** Byte counts are tool-result sizes, not tokens. Agent runs, added later the same day: Claude Code (Opus 5.5) on the mini, iPhone 17 Simulator, a page in Safari whose three switches are drawn on a canvas (the element list has the browser's buttons and one empty group), task "turn on the Lamp and the Heater, check, and say what the status line says". All four runs were right (the page's own log shows `on=011` after two taps) in 17 to 20 s. With `compare` only in the tool's description: three pictures, no `compare`. With a hint after each tap that redrew without an element change (`screenshot` with `compare: true` says what changed, as text): three runs, two or three pictures each, `compare` called once and only as an extra check that answered `same`. A model that sees pictures looks; a picture of this screen is about 470 tokens against about 60 for the report, and it trusted the picture. The hint cost about 28 tokens per tap and was removed. By hand on the same page `compare` gave the full answer: `~ "Everything is off" → "Running: Lamp" at 79,457`, `~ "Taps so far: 0" → "Taps so far: 1" at 67,489`, `region 296,256 72x40 (no text changed)`. So `compare` is for scripts, for checks without a model, and for models that cannot see; it does not change what a seeing agent does. A screenshot with `marks` is not kept as the picture to compare with. The text is only as good as the recognition: on the mini, where the Accurate level refuses (D64's limit), the Fast level read a signal icon as `5G .ll` and `7.00 GB` as `7.00 OB`, and the first read in a process took 14 to 17 s (later ones 0.2 to 0.3 s; the command line is a new process each time, the MCP server is not). A clock that moved a minute is reported (amended by D89: a time in the top tenth of the picture is left out). A scroll bar fading or the home indicator is a region with no text. Android was proven on stored screenshots only, since no emulator could be run today; Windows and Linux recognition were not run for this. Pictures taken with `--native` give pixels, not points. A change smaller than a cell's threshold (one thin line in an 8 by 8 cell: under 4 of 255 on average) is missed.

**Reopen if.** The text changes should come in the action's own reply on a page like that one (a web view whose content is drawn: today only a web view with no children at all is read from pixels), which would save the look and a turn; an agent run shows the lines mislead more than they save; the agent starts reporting the system bars' bounds for hollow screens (then drop them here too); a second recognition pass on the changed region alone would read small text better.

## D77. `boot` starts a simulator that is shut down, and "no device" names what can be started

**Choice.** `phonedriver boot` lists the iOS simulators that are shut down, newest system first; `phonedriver boot <name>` (the model in any case, or the id) starts one with `xcrun simctl bootstatus <udid> -b` and returns when it has booted. Over MCP it is the `devices` tool's `boot` argument, so it costs no eighteenth tool. A name a connected device has answers `already on`. With nothing connected, a request's `NoDevice` error carries `off` and reads `no device is connected; can be started: iPhone 17 (iOS 26.5)` with the hint to boot one, and `devices` prints the same line. It is a daemon method (`boot`, on `DeviceSource::off` and `DeviceSource::boot`), because the simulators are on the daemon's machine, which is not always the caller's.

**Why.** A new user with Xcode and nothing running got `no device is connected` and a doctor line that sent them to `xcrun simctl boot <udid>`. An agent that has only the MCP tools could not go on at all. Now the error says what exists and the next call starts it. A competitor (argent) boots simulators and emulators; this is the part of that gap that could be proven today.

**Rejected.** Starting a simulator without being asked when nothing is connected: it takes memory and seconds, and a user may have meant the phone that is not plugged in yet. Listing what is off in every `devices` reply: a Mac with Xcode has a dozen simulators, and they are noise once something is connected. Opening Simulator.app: the agent needs no window, and a daemon may run where there is no screen. A new tool: the tool list is re-read every turn. tvOS, watchOS and visionOS simulators: the runner is built for iOS. `NoDevice` for a name that nothing off has: its hint is about `--device`; that case is `Refused` with its own sentence.

**Evidence.** Tests written before the code: `with_nothing_connected_the_devices_that_are_off_are_named_with_how_to_start_one` (core), `the_simulators_that_are_off_are_the_ios_ones_that_can_boot_newest_system_first` (ios), `boot_lists_what_is_off_and_starts_one_by_name_or_id` and `with_nothing_connected_a_request_is_answered_with_what_can_be_started` (daemon), `devices_with_boot_starts_a_device_or_says_it_is_on_and_a_wrong_name_names_the_tool` (MCP), and the printing in `devices_with_nothing_connected_says_so_and_what_to_do`. They were first run after the code existed, so only two failures were seen: the daemon test file did not compile (`cannot find type DeviceSummary`), and the wrong-name wording failed once it was changed (`left: "no device \"Watch\"; connected: fake:1 (Fake), ..."`). Mac mini: core `--lib` 215 passed, ios `--lib` 68, cli `--lib` 111, `--test daemon` 177, `--test mcp` 46, `--test sources` 8, `--test dx` 6, `--test init` 8; clippy clean. Live on the mini with the iPhone 17 Simulator shut down by hand: `phonedriver boot` listed it; `phonedriver boot "iphone 17"` printed `booted sim:E02F... · iPhone 17 · iOS 26.5` in 4.5 s; a second call `already on:`; `boot Watch` `error: no device named "Watch" is off; can be started: iPhone 17 (iOS 26.5)`, exit 1. Through the real MCP server: `devices {"boot": "iPhone 17"}` 8.7 s, then `snapshot` of the home screen in 6.6 s (the runner starting).

**Limits.** Android emulators were not started in this decision; D79 adds them and changes `DeviceSource::boot` to take the name. The "nothing connected" error was proven with a fake source only: the mini always has two other simulators running that are not mine to stop. A simulator that has never booted was not tried (its first boot is slower). `bootstatus` has no time limit here. A physical phone that is switched off is not in the list.

**Reopen if.** An Android emulator host is available (then `boot` lists AVDs too, and this entry's hint for an empty list goes); users want the Simulator window opened; a first boot is seen to hang.

## D78. `press landscape` and `press portrait` turn the screen

**Choice.** Two more names for `press`: `landscape` and `portrait` (`Button::Landscape`, `Button::Portrait`). On iOS the runner sets `XCUIDevice.sharedDevice.orientation` to landscape-left or portrait. On Android the device runs `settings put system accelerometer_rotation 0; settings put system user_rotation 1` (or `0`) over adb. The reply is the usual observation, so its device line shows the new size and orientation.

**Why.** An agent asked to check a landscape layout, or to watch a video full screen, had no way to turn an iOS Simulator at all (`simctl` has no rotation), and on Android had to know two settings. A competitor (argent) rotates. `press` already holds things that are not keys (`fingerprint`), and a new name there costs no tool.

**Rejected.** A `rotate` verb and tool: one more tool for two words. Four orientations (upside down, landscape-right): no task has needed them. Remembering Android's auto-rotate setting and putting it back on `portrait`: state in the daemon for a setting the user can see and change; the reference says what happens. `cmd window user-rotation`: the two `settings` commands are the ones the rotation tests and bench scenarios here already run.

**Evidence.** `press_landscape_and_portrait_turn_the_screen_and_an_unknown_key_lists_them` (`--test daemon`) failed first with `no variant ... named Landscape`, then passed. Live on the iPhone 17 Simulator (iOS 26.5), Safari: `press landscape` -> `ok pressed landscape · settled in 808ms · new screen`, device line `874x402pt · landscape`, 1.3 s; the screenshot is 874x402 and I looked at it (Safari's landscape layout with its tab bar); `press portrait` -> `402x874pt · portrait`. A tap in landscape lands: `tap "Tabs"` -> `ok tapped @e21 "Tabs" at 780,32`, and the screenshot shows the tab overview. `landscape_turns_an_app_that_can_turn_and_portrait_turns_it_back` (`crates/ios/tests/device_live.rs`, against Safari because the testbed is portrait-only on iPhone) passed on that simulator in 9.9 s.

**Limits.** Android was run by hand once, later the same day, on the API 37 emulator on win-pc (Windows build): in Settings, `press landscape` -> device line `923x411pt · landscape`, the settings read `accelerometer_rotation 0`, `user_rotation 1`, and the 923x411 screenshot is on its side; `press portrait` -> `411x923pt · portrait`. The emulator was starved and showed `System UI isn't responding` throughout, so no tap was tried in landscape, and on the home screen (which does not turn) the reply was `no change after 504ms` with a hint about taps. Its test, `press_landscape_turns_the_screen_and_press_portrait_turns_it_back` (`crates/cli/tests/states.rs`), has still never run: the device test rig does not run on Windows. On a physical Android phone this changes the owner's auto-rotate setting and leaves it off. A lane (a hidden display) is not turned. A physical iPhone or iPad was not tried; on the CoreDevice tier without the runner the keys answer unsupported.

**Reopen if.** The Android test fails when an emulator is back; users need the other two orientations; a lane should turn.


**Amended 2026-11-17: an app that stays upright.** The runner turned each touch by the device's orientation. After `press landscape` the testbed, which stays upright on an iPhone, kept its 402x874 screen, and `tap "B5"` and `tap "B10"` answered `ok` but `Last tap` stayed `b2`: the touches went where the screen would have been had the app turned. The same holds for a person's iPhone held on its side with an app that does not turn. Now the touch is turned by the orientation of the app the last snapshot read (`XCUIApplication.interfaceOrientation`, the private property WebDriverAgent turns its taps by); a runner that has read no screen yet keeps the device's. On the iPhone 17 Simulator: in that state the three taps logged `b5`, `b10` and `b21`; Safari on its side (874x402) switched to the tapped tab `@e58 "Grace Hopper - Wikipedia"` (address `en.wikipedia.org`); a tap's `input` phase stayed at 353 to 369 ms. `a_tap_lands_in_an_upright_app_on_a_device_turned_on_its_side` (`crates/ios/tests/device_live.rs`) passes with the landscape and tap tests. An iPad upside down was not tried.
## D79. `boot` starts an Android virtual device too

**Choice.** The adb source lists the AVDs on the daemon's machine (`crates/android/src/avd.rs`: each `<name>.ini` in `ANDROID_AVD_HOME`, else `ANDROID_USER_HOME/avd`, else `~/.android/avd`, with the API level from its `target=android-37.0` line) and leaves out the ones a running emulator reports (`getprop ro.boot.qemu.avd_name`). `boot <name>` runs the SDK's `emulator -avd <name> -port <p>` detached from the daemon, on the first even port from 5554 that adb does not know and nothing listens on, so the serial is known before it exists, and returns `android:emulator-<p>` when `sys.boot_completed` is 1. If the emulator process ends first, the error carries its exit status and the last line it printed. For this, `DeviceSource::boot` now takes the name and resolves it itself (D77 had the daemon resolve it against `off`): a running AVD is not in `off`, and only its source can say it is already on.

**Why.** D77 left Android out because no emulator could run that day. An Android user with Android Studio and nothing running had the same dead end the iOS user had, and `emulator @<avd>` is a command few agents and fewer new users know.

**Rejected.** `emulator -list-avds`: newer emulators print information lines on the same stream, and the `.ini` files also give the API level. Finding the new emulator by comparing adb's list before and after: two boots at once would cross; a chosen port cannot. Asking the emulator console for the AVD name: it needs the auth token and does not work through a remote adb; the property is one `getprop`. `-no-window`, `-no-snapshot` or a variable for extra arguments: none was needed, also over ssh where no window can open (a variable for arguments was written and removed unrun). Waiting past `sys.boot_completed` until the agent connects: the only failures seen were on a host whose disk was starved, and there the wait would have been four minutes and ended on a system dialog.

**Evidence.** Three unit tests in `avd.rs` (folder, `.ini` files with CRLF as Windows writes them, where the program is found); `boot_lists_what_is_off_and_starts_one_by_name_or_id` still passes with the new trait shape; workspace on the Mac mini green except the known `no_device_exits_4...`; clippy clean. Live on win-pc (Windows 11, emulator 37.2.13, Windows build of this tree, over ssh), 2026-11-16: `phonedriver boot` listed the four AVDs above; `phonedriver devices` with nothing connected printed `no devices` / `can be started: Pixel_API_37 (Android API 37), ...` / `hint: Start one with \`phonedriver boot <name>\`.` (the first run of that message against a real machine); `phonedriver boot pixel_api_37` started `emulator.exe -avd Pixel_API_37 -port 5554` as a child of the daemon; that first boot was cold and took 13 minutes on a disk another build was using (adb said `offline` for 8 of them), and my client had given up after 4. Then `boot pixel_api_37` -> `already on: android:emulator-5554 · Pixel_API_37 · Android API 37`, and `boot` listed the other three. After `adb emu kill`, `phonedriver boot Pixel_API_37` -> `booted android:emulator-5554 · Pixel_API_37 · Android API 37` in 6.3 s (the emulator's saved state). `phonedriver daemon stop`, then `tasklist` still showed `qemu-system-x86_64.exe` and adb still listed `emulator-5554 device`. `ro.boot.qemu.avd_name` read `Pixel_API_37` on API 37.

**Limits.** The cold boot's `booted` line was never seen, only the resumed one's. After the cold boot the first `snapshot` failed for four minutes (`Timeout while connecting UiAutomation`, then `disconnected`) with the guest's io pressure at 80 to 97 percent, and the launcher and System UI stopped responding: `boot` returns when Android says it booted, which on a starved host is before it can be driven. Not run: macOS and Linux (the process-group detach there is untested), API 26 and 31 (the property name is confirmed on API 37 only; on an image that lacks it a running AVD would be listed as off and `boot` would start a second copy, which the emulator refuses), an emulator that fails to start, two boots at once. The emulator is Google's program and may report crashes or metrics as its own settings say; phonedriver adds no flag for or against that. The run left a saved state taken while System UI was not responding; I deleted it (`snapshots/default_boot` of Pixel_API_37), so the next boot there is cold.

**Reopen if.** A healthy host also hands back a device that cannot be driven yet; an older image lacks the property; users want `-no-window` or a cold boot.

## D80. On a phone the site is laid out for a thumb

These two were published on `main` as D70 and D71 while this branch had its own D70 (a secret is masked) and D71 (a web page read as text). They are renumbered here at the merge; `site/README.md` follows.

**Choice.** (1) Below 700 px the hero's controls run in the order they act on: the setup line (two lines, the whole bar copies), its client tabs, then Get started. The player bar (play, chapter, clock, scrubber) leads the session log and pins to the bottom of the window from the moment the phone reaches the upper half of the screen until the log scrolls in, so the recording and what it is doing are seen together. The log is the full width of the screen and does not scroll under a finger. The two figures under it are two tiles. Get started is an outline, so the setup line is the one filled control on the first screen. The phone is never taller than the window less 150 px, and on a phone held sideways it fits the window's height. (2) Below 1000 px the dock shows the current stop, its number of eight and a dotted line that fills as the page is read; tapping it opens the eight stops with their questions as a sheet from the top, the same sheet as the menu. (3) Below 900 px the read works by tap: a row of the screenshot puts its line in a readout pinned under the screenshot, and a line unfolds the strip of the screenshot it stands for. (4) Hover styles apply only where there is a hover; on touch the same looks are the pressed state, and the grey tap flash is off. Controls a thumb aims at are 44 px tall (inline text links, the scrubber at 34 px, the dock's Copy at 40 px and the code blocks' Copy at 38 px are not). (5) A tapped row in an exclusive accordion stays where it was tapped while the open one closes (D81 has how). (6) On a phone the setup prompt has a second button that opens the share sheet with the prompt and the address. (7) The docs list is one row that scrolls sideways, opened at the current page.

**Why.** The user called the site clumsy on a phone. At 390 px the client tabs sat under Get started, away from the line they change; the log was a screen below the recording it narrates; "Point at a line" had no pointer and its two halves were a screen apart; opening an FAQ row moved it 103 px from the finger; a tapped button stayed orange; twenty-odd controls were 26 to 38 px tall; three pane titles were cut off; and the dock named a stop without offering the others on an 18,000 px page. A visitor on a phone cannot install anything there, so the useful end of the visit is getting the prompt to a computer.

**Limits.** Checked in Chrome and in Playwright's WebKit with touch emulation at 320, 360 and 390 px, light and dark, not on a real phone and not in Safari itself. The pinned bar relies on `overflow:clip` on the hero (Safari 16 and later); before that the bar sits in place above the log. `@media (scripting)` hides the bar before the phone arrives; browsers without it show the bar from the first screen. The share sheet was not opened in a test, since headless browsers have none; without `navigator.share` the button copies. `navigator.vibrate` on copy does nothing in Safari. The tablet width (700 to 1019 px) keeps the old stacked hero. Frame times were measured with the CPU slowed four times, which does not slow the GPU. A window under 520 px tall has no pinned readout in the read.

**Reopen if.** The pinned bar hides something on a real phone's toolbar layout, or people tap the phone to pause and miss the bar.

## D81. Nothing on the site changes size or place in one frame

**Choice.** (1) Every `<details>` opens and closes by its own height, driven by `site/lib/fold-engine.ts`, not by `::details-content` transitions. An exclusive list keeps its `name` in the HTML; the engine takes it off at the first tap and closes the other answer itself, so both move together, and it holds the tapped row at the same place on the screen. (2) A link to a place on the same page more than two screens away fades to it in a view transition (`site/lib/jump-engine.ts`); nearer ones still scroll. The move is `location.hash`, and Back after it is a cut. (3) The install box eases between panels of different heights and the new panel fades in (`site/lib/tabs-engine.ts`); the hero's setup line fades in when its tab changes. (4) A button keeps its width when its label becomes "Copied". (5) A tap on the read during its scan ends the scan and selects the row. (6) The menu's and the route map's backdrop fades. (7) The answers take 300 ms and the install box 280 ms: both move layout on every frame, so they stay at the 300 ms ceiling for interface motion; they were 420 and 360 ms for a day. (8) Nothing that moves per scroll frame is driven by a custom property set on a parent: the dock's dotted line is its own element with its own `clip-path`.

**Why.** The user asked for the site to feel seamless. Measured at 390 px in Chrome and in Playwright's WebKit before the change: Copy buttons went from 64 to 80 px in the hero and 109 to 70 px in the dock; the install box went from 450 to 180 px in one frame; a jump from the route map to the last stop scrolled 13,700 px in 1.5 s in Chrome, starting every stop's animation on the way, and cut in WebKit; a closing answer vanished in one frame in WebKit; the read ignored taps for the 3.7 s of its scan. Layout shift while loading was already zero and page-to-page navigation already took under 200 ms with a transition, so those were left alone.

**Limits.** With JavaScript off, or with reduced motion on, `<details>` behaves natively: exclusive, not animated. `history.pushState` cannot be used for the jump, because the router answers it with a page transition of its own (seen: `AbortError: Transition was skipped`). A browser without view transitions scrolls as before. In WebKit the jump lands 45 px higher than in Chrome (the section's top at 43 px, not 88 px); the heading is 124 px below that, so it is in view in both. Measured in headless browsers, not on a phone.

**Reopen if.** A real phone shows the held row trembling while an answer above it folds (then close the other answer first), or the fade feels like a page change (then shorten it or scroll in steps).

## D82. The home page says each thing once, and reference lives in the docs

**Choice.** Each stop on the home page is a heading, a lede of at most two sentences and one sentence of caption per figure. (1) The approval stop keeps three facts (the three answers, the eleven languages and what they miss, password fields) in the left column under the lede, beside the recorded prompt, and one link to the approval guard doc; `PHONEDRIVER_ASK`, `autonomous: true` and `PHONEDRIVER_GUARD=off` are in that doc only. (2) "How it works" ends at the device table and the tool list; no cable, slow links and the 30-second hand-over are in the phone guides and the changelog only. (3) The limits are five one-thought items. (4) The home page shows the four questions in `FAQ`; the six search-shaped ones are on `/faq/` only, and that page's structured data picks them up because it lists every question the home page does not. (5) A caption does not repeat a number its figure already shows.

**Why.** The user asked whether the page was too wordy and then for it to be as clean as possible. Captured on the Mac mini before the change: 18 screens at 1440x900 and 27 at 390x844, about 2,190 words in view, the node and line counts stated three times in the read stop, "the human said no, nothing was charged" twice in a row, and five fact blocks under the approval prompt in a second band while the column beside the prompt stood empty.

**Limits.** The home page gives up the six search-shaped questions as on-page text and in its own FAQ structured data; they stay indexed on `/faq/`. The approval lede names four of the seven guarded labels. Judged from headless captures in dark, light, tablet and phone widths, not by a person on a phone.

**Reopen if.** Search traffic to the home page for those six questions drops, or a reader asks on the page for something that moved to the docs.

**Reopened the same day.** The home page shows five questions: "Is there an alternative to mobile-mcp?" is back. On 14 November Brave ranked the home page fifth for "mobile-mcp alternative", and on 16 November Google had not yet crawled `/compare/mobile-mcp/` (Search Console: discovered, not indexed), so the home page is the only indexed page that answers it. The tool names in the token chart and "adb" in the speed lede now link to their comparison pages; before, the home page had no link to `/compare/mobile-mcp/`. Take the question off the home page again once the comparison page ranks for that query.

## D83. The race runs at real speed on request, a copied prompt says where it goes, and the read is six lines on a phone

**Choice.** (1) The race has a two-way switch, "25x slower" and "Real speed" (`site/lib/race-engine.ts`). Slowed, each ball loops on its own trip time as before. At real speed all eight leave together every 4.7 s (the slowest trip plus 1.1 s of rest); a ball that is back lights its figure, and a trip under 60 ms, too short to draw, leaves a streak along its lane. Choosing a speed starts the race even with reduced motion on, since the reader asked for it. (2) After the setup prompt is copied, the page says "Copied. Paste it into your agent." with the marks of the eight clients the docs cover. In the hero the chauffeur already says that line, so the marks appear under it; from the dock and the install box it is a notice under the dock for 4.2 s (`site/components/Paste.tsx`, `copy-engine.ts`). A copied shell command or config gets no notice. (3) Below 900 px the snapshot in the read shows its first six lines and a "Show all 14 lines" row, which opens by its height in 300 ms and does not close again (`views-engine.ts`). The line selected at rest is then `@e4`, the last one in view.

**Why.** The user picked these three from a list of ideas. A reader told "170x" learns a number; watching phonedriver's ball come home before adb's has left the start is the same fact felt. Copying the prompt is the page's one job and "Copied" did not say what to do with it. The read was five screens on a phone.

**Limits.** The marks are from Simple Icons 16.38.0 (CC0 data); each is its owner's trademark, used to name a client. Only Claude Code is a client I test with, and the docs say so. The streak stands for a trip the screen cannot show: at 60 Hz a 9 ms trip is under one frame. With JavaScript off there is no switch, no notice and all 14 lines.

**Reopen if.** A trademark owner objects to a mark, or the real-speed round reads as broken (four orange balls that never seem to move) to someone who has not read the caption.

## D84. A signed runner whose profile has run out is built again by the next command

**Choice.** A device build of the runner is used again only while its signing profile is valid. `build_signed` (`crates/ios/src/runner.rs`) reads `ExpirationDate` from `embedded.mobileprovision` in the build's products (the file is a signed envelope with its property list inside as plain text, so no `security cms` is run) and, when that date has passed, builds again with `-allowProvisioningUpdates`; `xcodebuild test-without-building` then installs the new build over the one on the phone. `doctor` reads the same file of this version's build: `signed, profile expires <date>`, or a warning that the profile ran out and that the next command signs again. A build of other sources is removed after a new build, for devices as it already was for the Simulator. `UserAction::ResignRunner` is removed: nothing ever returned it, and its hint named a command that did not sign anything.

**Why.** A user reported the seven-day expiry of a free Apple ID's signature as a limit, and the README answered that `phonedriver ios setup` signs again. It did not: an existing build was always used again, so after seven days the only way out was to delete `~/.phonedriver/runner` by hand. Apple sets the seven days; who has to remember them is ours to choose.

**Limits.** Seen on a MacBook with a paid team (the profile lasts a year), with the cached profile's date changed by hand to the day before: `doctor` warned, the next `ios setup` built and signed again in 8.4 s, the profile read 2027-11-12 again, the build of the previous sources (53 MB) was gone, and the command after that built nothing. Not seen: a free Apple ID, where Xcode has to issue a new seven-day profile during that build, and the install on the phone, which was locked. The runner is signed again only when the date has passed, so a session that starts in a profile's last hour can lose its runner part way. A runner that is already running and answers `hello` is used without looking at the date. The phone must be attached and unlocked for the install, as for the first one.

**Rejected.** Signing again a day early: Xcode keeps a profile that is still valid, so the build would repeat on every command of the last day. A background timer that signs while nobody uses the phone: it would build and install on a phone nobody asked it to touch.

**Reopen if.** A free Apple ID's build after expiry fails where the paid team's did not (then the fix for that failure goes here), or people lose sessions in a profile's last hour.

## D85. What a run of simulator-use's benchmark steps changed: late ids, row ids, the app node, a zero settle time, and a secret in an MCP batch

**Choice.** Five changes, each from a line that phonedriver printed and should not have, found by running the steps of simulator-use's published benchmark (its `bench/run.mjs`, its `examples/swiftui-demo` app) through `phonedriver mcp` with `bench/simulator-use/probe.py`.
(1) An element with a label that gains or loses its app id where it stands keeps its ref (`core::refs::assign`). (2) A row with no id of its own that takes its words from one text takes that text's id (`core::prune`). (3) iOS's application node is not listed. (4) A snapshot's header says `settled in Nms` only when N is more than zero. (5) The MCP `batch` tool passes a `type` step's `secret` to the daemon, and `type` with `secret` takes `then`; the command line still refuses a secret in a batch, because a secret never goes on argv.

**Why.** On an iPhone 17 Simulator (iOS 26.5) the first tap by ref on the demo's sign-in screen answered `- @e8 @e10 @e11` / `+ @e12 cell "Invalid email or password"` / `+ @e13 button "Login" selected` / `+ @e14 button "Items"`: the two tab-bar buttons had gained the identifiers `person` and `list.bullet` since the first read, and the status row, a `Form` cell around `Text(message).accessibilityIdentifier("status")`, had no id because the id sits on the text the row absorbs. With (1) and (2) the reply is `~ @e8 cell "Not signed in" → "Invalid email or password"`. Every iOS screen began `@e1 group "<app name>"` with everything else one level in, which the header's `app` line already says. A batch that fills a sign-in form and submits it is the commonest batch there is, and it was refused at its password step; the daemon has masked a batch step's secret since D70 and its property test generates that step, so only the MCP server's refusal stood in the way.

**Measured.** Same Simulator, debug build, Mac mini, 2026-11-16, tokens as characters / 4, medians: 20 reads, 20 taps, 10 types, 10 sign-ins of each kind, every one checked by its text.

| Step | Before | After | simulator-use 0.15.1, its page |
|---|---|---|---|
| Read the screen | 82 tokens, 0.17 s | 69 tokens, 0.12 s | 72 tokens, 0.30 s |
| Tap, result shown | 41 tokens, 0.91 s | 28 tokens, 0.83 s | 17 tokens, 0.72 s |
| Type an email, text shown | 60 tokens, 1.32 s | 38 tokens, 1.33 s | 14 tokens, 1.21 s |
| Sign in, five calls | 300 tokens, 4.06 s | 254 tokens, 4.03 s | 194 tokens, 4.28 s |
| Sign in, batched | refused at the password | 247 tokens, 4.04 s | 179 tokens, 3.62 s |
| Tool list and instructions | 1,914 tokens | 1,914 tokens | 2,443 tokens |

**Limits.** The last column is from simulator-use.dev/benchmarks as read on 2026-11-16 (their run of 2026-11-06 on a virtual M4 with iOS 27.0), not a run of that tool here, so its times are not comparable with these and only its token counts are. This is not the published comparison: that needs their harness run whole, with phonedriver as one more tool, on one machine, after the release. phonedriver still answers an action with more text than simulator-use does: the summary line with its place and settle time, the keyboard, and what the keyboard covers. A password field's change is still two lines (`secure field changed` and its focus). Rule (1) is for elements with a label; an unlabeled element that gains an id still gets a new ref. Rule (2) gives an Android row the resource id of its one text, which many rows share; nothing uses a shared id to match. The Android emulator suite was not run on these changes: win-pc's emulator stopped answering `adb shell` under that machine's other builds. In an MCP batch the secret is in the tool call's arguments, as it is for a `type` on its own.

**Rejected.** Dropping `under-keyboard` from the children of a covered group, and dropping the changed field's own line after a verified `type`: both save tokens on this benchmark and both take away something an agent uses on other screens. Letting the command line's `batch` carry a secret: its steps are arguments, and arguments show in `ps`.

**Amended by D87** (2026-11-16): both rejected cuts were made in a narrower form, with what they took away put back another way.

**Reopen if.** A label that repeats at one place under two different owners trades refs because of rule (1), or the full harness run puts phonedriver's sign-in above 250 tokens.

## D86. An Android phone in use stays awake, on its own screen and in hidden mode

**Choice.** The agent's monitor (`Monitor.java`) calls `IPowerManager.userActivity` for display 0 every 10 s (`NUDGE_MS`) while three things hold: the host sent a request in the last 10 minutes (`KEEP_AWAKE_MS`, a session's hold, as `KEEP_AWAKE` in D56), the phone is unlocked, and its own screen is on. Each control connection stamps its last request (`Control.usedAt`), and a lane with no connection is never nudged, so `phonedriver release`, which drops the device and ends the connection, stops it at once and the phone sleeps and locks on its own timer. It acts on display 0 whatever display the lane works on, so it is the same for the phone's own screen and for a hidden display. The shell user holds `DEVICE_POWER`.

**Why.** Seen on the HONOR X8 on 2026-11-16: the phone locked about 30 s after its owner's last touch while an agent worked on the hidden display, because input on a display in its own group does not reset the phone's own screen timeout (D32), and every call after that said the phone was locked. On the phone's own screen an agent that reads or thinks for longer than the timeout came back to a dark phone, locked if it has a PIN, as an iPhone did before D56. The user asked for it the same evening.

**Rejected.** `svc power stayon` and `stay_on_while_plugged_in`: they change the owner's setting, and it outlives the session when the host goes away without undoing it. A screen wake lock: it must be released on every way the agent ends, where a nudge that stops coming needs no undoing. A key event, as D56 does on iOS: `userActivity` carries no event, so no app sees anything. The host deciding and sending an op: the agent already polls the lock every 500 ms and sees every request, so the host would add a timer and an op for the same decision.

**Evidence.** HONOR X8 (Android 13, PIN, screen timeout 30 s), nobody touching it, 2026-11-16. The live test `a_phone_in_use_has_its_screen_timeout_reset_every_ten_seconds` (the bare agent connected, one `state` request, then 12 s with no request) failed with the agent before this change (`lastUserActivityTime` 10812571 both times) and passed with it. Through the CLI, `launch Settings` on the hidden display and then no call for 60 s: at 15, 30, 45 and 60 s the phone was unlocked and awake and its last user activity 2 to 8 s old. The same on the phone's own screen. After `release` no user activity followed, and the phone slept and locked 30 s after the last nudge. A `press home` on the locked phone woke its screen to the keyguard and answered `The device is locked`; with the host still connected the screen went off by itself within 12 s, so nothing nudged a locked phone. Not run: an emulator (win-pc's belonged to another session), Android before 12 (the other `userActivity` signature), a 15 s timeout.

**Cost.** As D56: a phone left with a session, or after one terminal command with no `release`, stays on and unlocked for up to 10 minutes after the last call. In hidden mode the owner's own screen stays on as well, dimmed (amendment below), on whatever the owner left there.

**Amended 2026-11-17: the screen dims.** The nudge passes `USER_ACTIVITY_FLAG_NO_CHANGE_LIGHTS`, so it resets the screen-off timer without brightening the screen: the screen dims on the owner's schedule and stays dim, on, and unlocked, instead of staying bright for 10 minutes. The user asked why the owner's screen stayed lit in hidden mode. Evidence, the API 37 emulator on win-pc with `stay_on_while_plugged_in` 0 and a 15 s timeout, `press home` and then no call: bright until 5 s, dimmed at 10 s (`policy=DIM`, brightness 0.05), and still awake and dim at 60 s with `lastUserActivityTime` 70 s old and `lastUserActivityTimeNoChangeLights` refreshed every 10 s; 5 s after `release` the screen was off and at 10 s the emulator was asleep. Not yet run on a physical phone.

## D87. A reply says each thing once

**Choice.** Eight cuts to what replies print, each a line or a word that repeated something the agent already had.
(1) A header names the keyboard only when it is shown, and a settle time only when the screen did not settle (`not settled after Nms`). An action's first line no longer says how long it waited.
(2) A diff says the keyboard's coming and going in one line: `keyboard shown over @e8 @e9`, naming the elements it now covers, or `keyboard hidden`. Before, it was a `+` line for the keyboard and a `~ … → under-keyboard` line for each element.
(3) `under-keyboard` is printed on the outermost covered element only, and holds for the lines under it. A child the keyboard leaves uncovered inside a covered parent says `above-keyboard` (`Rendered::cover_word`).
(4) The keyboard's own element has no line.
(5) A container whose line would only name its role (`@e2 list`, the table iOS puts around a form) has no line, and its children print one level up (`Rendered::listed`).
(6) After a verified `type`, the diff leaves out what the first line already says: the field taking focus, a secure field's `secure field changed`, and, after a replace, the field's new text (`core::diff::drop_echo`). A changed label, appended text and every other element stay.
(7) A tap on a named element is summarised as `tapped @e3 "Three"`. The point it landed on moves to the reply's `at` field and to the recording's steps file.
(8) A type into a field is summarised as `typed into @e4`. The character count is kept only for a type with no field.

**Why.** After D85, simulator-use's sign-in still cost 254 tokens against its 194, and every extra token was a repeat.
- A header said `keyboard hidden` on screens with no keyboard, and `settled in 212ms` on every read.
- A reply that showed the keyboard come up listed each element it covered, and then the keyboard itself.
- A verified type of `gary@example.com` said `typed 16 chars into @e3 · verified` and then `~ @e3 textbox (none) → "gary@example.com"`.
- A secure field said `secure field changed` under a line that said it was typed and verified.
- The form's `@e2 list` line took a line and indented five more.

D85 rejected two of these cuts, because each took away something an agent uses. Both came back in a form that keeps it.
- The children of a covered group are still known to be covered: the word holds for the lines under it, and an exception is said.
- The changed field's line goes only when the first line has already said it: `verified` after a replace means the field holds exactly the text sent.

**Measured.** Same Simulator, app and probe as D85 (`bench/simulator-use/probe.py`), Mac mini, 2026-11-17, debug build, medians of 20 reads, 20 taps, 10 types and 10 of each sign-in, every one checked by its text. The probe now also counts a type as shown when its reply says `verified`, since the reply no longer repeats the text.

| Step | D85 | Now | simulator-use 0.15.1, its page |
|---|---|---|---|
| Read the screen | 69 tokens | 59 tokens | 72 tokens |
| Tap, result shown | 28 tokens | 20 tokens | 17 tokens |
| Type an email | 38 tokens | 13 tokens | 14 tokens |
| Sign in, five calls | 254 tokens | 176 tokens | 194 tokens |
| Sign in, batched | 247 tokens | 177 tokens | 179 tokens |
| Tool list and instructions | 1,914 tokens | 1,914 tokens | 2,443 tokens |

**Limits.**
- The last column is still simulator-use's published numbers, not a run of that tool here (D85's limit).
- A tap that changes one row costs 3 tokens more than theirs: its `~` line says the row's old text as well as the new.
- Two runs of the sign-in can differ by a few tokens per call, depending on whether the keyboard was up when a reply was read.
- A secure field's change is not said after a verified type. An unverified one is still said, with what the field shows.
- `at` is in the JSON reply only. The command line prints it with `--json`.

**Rejected.** Dropping a `~` line's old value (`~ @e7 cell "Invalid email or password"`): it would win the single tap, but the old value is what lets an agent see which of two similar rows changed without reading the screen again. Not printing the keyboard at all: an agent needs to know that part of the screen is covered.

## D88. On macOS, Vision reads at the Fast level only

**Choice.** `recognize` on macOS reads at Vision's Fast level only, with the picture enlarged to about three pixels a point through Core Image first, as D64's fallback did. The Accurate level, its revision 2, the fallback that remembered a refusal for the rest of the process, and `recognize_at` are gone.

**Why.** The Accurate level needs a model that macOS 27.0.1 compiles on a process's first call, and the command line is a new process for every `compare`. The user asked why `compare` took 21 to 31 s the first time on a Mac and 10 to 15 s every time on the Mac mini. A probe that times `VNRecognizeTextRequest` in a fresh process, 2026-11-16: on the MacBook (M4 Pro, macOS 27.0.1) the Accurate level's first call took 28 s and the next ones 146 ms; the Fast level's first call took 41 to 56 ms and the next ones 20 to 23 ms. On the Mac mini the Accurate level failed every call (`nilError`) after 12 to 17 s, whatever the compute device or renderer, and the Fast level read. On the four pictures the tests use (the testbed's taps tab at one and two pixels a point, Android's Settings, the iOS testbed's gestures tab) the Fast level found what D64 records the Accurate level finding, except `Busy`, read as `Bus`: 23 of the taps tab's 24 two-character labels exact at one pixel a point, and 21 at two.

**Rejected.** Warming the Accurate model up in the daemon: `compare` runs in its own process, and the warm-up would still cost a Mac 28 s of compute the first time and the mini a 12 to 17 s refusal at every daemon start. Pinning Vision to the CPU and giving it a software Core Image context (`setComputeDevice`, `kCIContextUseSoftwareRenderer`): the same speed on the MacBook, and on the mini they did not stop the stalls under Limits. A lock so that one Vision call runs at a time: the stalls came on one thread too.

**Evidence.** `phonedriver compare` (debug build) on the MacBook, a new process each time, on pairs of the iOS testbed's screenshots: 0.27 to 1.17 s for two different tabs, the slowest the taps and text tabs (`+ "Plain" at 76,185`, `- "Last tap: none" at 302,352` and twelve more lines), and 0.15 s and `same` for a picture against itself, which reads no text. On the mini, `--test ocr`: the five reading tests pass.

**Limits.** The Fast level misreads small, low-contrast text more often than the Accurate level did: `B0` came out `BO` on an iOS screenshot at 1.5 pixels a point. On the Mac mini, which was swapping (3.6 of 4 GB) while another session compiled Metal shaders, single Vision calls stalled for 172 to 256 s with the CPU idle, waiting in Core Image renders, at both levels and with every setting tried. The speed test (`a_frame_of_412_by_915_takes_under_120_ms_at_the_median`) fails there for that reason (median 174 ms, one call 177 s). Not seen on the MacBook.

**Reopen if.** Agent runs show the Fast level's misreads costing taps, or a later macOS caches the Accurate model so that its first call is fast.

## D89. A replay checks each step's screen against a kept screenshot, by its text

**Choice.** `phonedriver replay <file> --screens <dir>` takes a screenshot after each step that worked. When `<dir>` has none for the step (`01.png`, `02.png`, numbered from the file's first step) it keeps this one there; when it has one, it compares the two with `compare` (D76) and stops at the first step whose text changed, or whose text could not be read where pixels changed, with the report and the hint to delete the kept file if the new screen is right. Pixels that changed with no text in them do not stop it; the step's line says `screen: 1 region changed, no text`. A screenshot of another size stops it. `compare` now leaves out a status bar clock that moved: a change whose text on both sides is a time (`9:41`, `21:05`, `9:41 PM`) and whose box is centred in the top tenth of the picture, and the regions it was the only text change in. The GitHub Action takes `screens`, a folder with a subfolder for each run named as its steps file; a run with no subfolder keeps its screens in the artifact under `screens/`, and on Linux the Action installs Tesseract first. Not in MCP: it is for CI and for people checking a build, and the tool list stays as it is.

**Why.** A replay proved that the taps still land and the waited-for text still shows; it did not see a screen that went wrong around them. Every visual regression tool compares pictures, and pixel comparisons of real screens fail on noise: a video encoder's screenshots of one still screen differ (D76), a scroll bar fades, a row stays pressed. Text decides here, as in `compare`, and the failure says what changed in words a person or an agent can act on, not a diff image. Keeping the first screenshots without a flag is the snapshot tests' way: the first run writes, later runs compare, and deleting a file accepts a new screen.

**Rejected.** Failing on pixel changes alone: on the iPhone 17 Simulator the screen after the tap on General differed from its kept screenshot in one region on one replay and in two on the next, with no text changed, and step 4's report had `region 392,120 8x640`, the scroll bar. Hiding the status bar by height: an iPad's navigation title sits inside the top tenth, so only clock-shaped text is left out there. Overriding the status bar (`simctl status_bar override`, Android's demo mode): it changes the device, and a phone belongs to a person. A separate `--update` flag: deleting the file does it.

**Evidence.** Unit tests: `a_status_bar_clock_that_moved_is_left_out_and_a_time_lower_down_is_not` (it fails with the filter turned off: the clock's region stays in `left`), and `a_wait_step_is_described_as_the_command_that_waits` (it printed `wait {"condition":...}` before). `--test cli replay_with_screens_keeps_each_steps_screen_then_compares_with_it` passes through the binary and a fake daemon: it keeps two screens, matches them, keeps a deleted one again and stops at a screenshot of another size. Live on the iPhone 17 Simulator (iOS 26.5), a debug build on the Mac mini, `testbeds/ios/runs/settings.steps`: the first replay kept five screens; the next stopped at step 4 with `~ "1.68 GB" → "1.64 GB" at 336,725` (the About page shows the Mac's free disk, which another process was filling); after that file was deleted and step 5's file replaced with step 3's (General's page), it stopped at step 5 with `~ "General" → "About" at 201,85`, `+ "Name" at 59,161`, ten more lines and `and 20 more`, in 31 s on the mini while it swapped. The Action's replay step, run by hand on the mini against the Simulator: a first pass with no kept folder kept two screens under `phonedriver-runs/screens/<run>`, a second pass given that folder matched both.

**Limits.** Text that changes on its own (free space, a date, a counter, the keyboard's suggestions) stops a replay; the report names it, but there is no way yet to leave a region out. The check is only as good as the recognition (D88's misreads). A clock in an app's own content inside the top tenth is left out too. One screenshot per step: a screen still animating when the step returns can differ between replays. The status bar's battery level on a phone is not left out.

**Reopen if.** Users need to leave a region out (a `--mask` with points from `compare`'s report), or text that changes on its own makes most failures noise.

**Amended 2026-11-17: the status bar, and a screen still moving.** The Action's Android job failed on its second pass: `+ "O" at 51,25` and `region 336,16 48x16`, a notification icon that had come into the status bar between the runs. Now the device says where its status bar ends and the check leaves out what changed wholly above that line, text and regions, before any text is read. The Android agent already skipped the status bar window (a full-width system window on the top edge) and now reports its bottom edge as `bar`; the iOS runner asks SpringBoard for its status bar once for each width of the app and reports `bar` with the snapshot. The daemon keeps it with the last tree it read and gives it with a screenshot as `status_bar`, in rows of the picture (55 on the API 37 emulator at 411x923, 54 on the iPhone 17 Simulator at 402x874). This is not the rejected hiding by height: the height is the device's own, so an iPad's title below its status bar is still compared, and a screen with no status bar leaves nothing out. `compare` of two files still leaves out only the clock. Second, a screen that differs is looked at again. Fifteen passes of the testbed's `taps.steps` against kept screens on the API 37 emulator failed four times, none of them a real change: three times `+ "q•" at 392,116`, the switch's press ripple still around its thumb (tiles of the step's screenshots show it in some and not others), and once `~ "Text" → "Texl"` and `~ "b10" → "blO"` on a blocky picture just after the restart (the encoder's first frame after a large change; its 8-pixel cells differed from the sharp one by up to 22 of 255, two sharp frames by at most 1.9). Now a screen that differs is taken again every 250 ms, compared again whenever its pixels moved, and reported only when it has stayed the same for 1.5 s or 5 s have passed; a screen about to be kept is taken until two in a row look the same. A real change costs up to 1.5 s more before the replay stops, and a passing step costs nothing more.

Evidence, a debug build on the Mac mini, 2026-11-17. API 37 emulator: after the change, one pass kept three screens and ten passes against them all matched (7.4 to 14.1 s each); `compare` of a kept screenshot and the same with `VPN 5G` drawn into its status bar reports `+ "VPN 5G" at 160,24`, while a replay against that kept file matched all three screens; a kept file with `Planted` drawn just below the status bar stopped the replay with `- "Planted" at 249,70` after 9 s. iPhone 17 Simulator: `compare` reports `+ "LTE42%" at 192,30` for `LTE 42%` drawn into the status bar, a replay against it matched, and `Planted` just below it stopped the replay with `- "Planted" at 191,70`. Unit tests: `what_changed_inside_the_status_bar_is_left_out_and_what_reaches_below_it_is_not`, `a_picture_is_still_when_nothing_below_the_status_bar_moved`, `the_status_bar_the_agent_left_out_is_kept_as_its_bottom_edge_in_points`, `the_status_bar_springboard_reported_is_kept_with_the_tree`. Limits: a screenshot taken before any screen was read has no status bar to leave out; the Android hidden display has no status bar, so nothing is left out there; a change that moves for longer than 5 s is reported as it was at the end.

## D90. `snapshot --audit` lists accessibility problems from the element list

**Choice.** `snapshot --audit` (MCP: `snapshot` with `audit: true`, hidden from the lean schema and named in the tool's description) appends `audit: N findings` and a line for each problem, at most 20 (`crates/core/src/audit.rs`). Two checks. A control, meaning an element that can be tapped or typed into or has a control's role (button, switch, checkbox, radio, slider, field, tab, menu item), with no label, no hint and no text inside it: `@e1 button: no name a screen reader can say`. A tap target under 44 by 44 points on iOS (Apple's Human Interface Guidelines) or 48 by 48 on Android (the Material and Android accessibility guidelines): `@e3 button "Close": 24x24pt, under 48x48pt`; a target that touches the screen's edge or the edge of a scrollable element around it is not measured, since only part of it may be on screen, and neither is a link. With no problem the line is `audit: no unnamed controls or small tap targets`.

**Why.** An agent that walks an app for a developer can check each screen it passes, and the element list it already reads holds what the two most common findings of Apple's Accessibility Inspector and Google's Accessibility Scanner need. The European Accessibility Act has applied to many consumer apps (shopping, banking, travel) since June 2025.

**Rejected.** Colour contrast and cut-off text: they need the pixels and OCR, and a misread would be reported as a finding. Two controls with the same name: a list's rows each have a "Delete", which is right, and the tree does not say which repeat is a mistake. A tool of its own: it would cost every agent the tokens of its description on every turn.

**Evidence.** `audit::tests::a_control_with_nothing_to_say_and_a_small_target_are_found_and_the_rest_is_not` (an unnamed image button, a 24 by 24 close button, an unnamed field found; a named row, a field with a hint and a row cut off by its list not found) fails with the cut-off rule turned off (`"@e8 button \"Last row\": 360x10pt, under 48x48pt"` appears); `an_iphone_asks_for_44_points`; `--test cli snapshot_audit_adds_the_screens_accessibility_problems` through the binary and a fake daemon. The MCP tool-list budget test passes. Live on the iPhone 17 Simulator (iOS 26.5): Settings, 2 findings (the search field, 38 points high, and its Dictate button, 17 by 22); Maps, 7 (among them the More button at 27 by 27 and the profile button at 42 by 42); Calendar and Photos, none.

**Limits.** The size is the element's frame; a control whose touch area is larger than its frame (Android's `TouchDelegate`, an iOS hit-test override, React Native's `hitSlop`) is reported though a finger reaches it. So when there is a small-target finding, the audit ends with one line that says so ("sizes are the bounds accessibility reports; a touch area the app enlarges (React Native `hitSlop`) is not seen", issue #43). It is said for every app: a React Native view reports `android.view.ViewGroup` or `android.widget.TextView` as its class, so the tree does not tell a React Native app from another. A control whose name comes only from an image the screen reader recognises is reported as unnamed. Elements hidden from the accessibility tree are not seen at all, which is itself a problem the audit cannot report.

**Reopen if.** Developers ask for contrast or duplicate-name checks, or a finding proves wrong on a common control.

## D91. The iOS runner's Xcode project ships with phonedriver, and its session records no video

**Choice.** `ios-runner/PhonedriverRunner.xcodeproj` (the project file and the shared scheme, generated once by XcodeGen 2.46 with `projectFormat: xcode15_0`, so object version 60, which Xcode 15 and later open) is in the repository and in the binary's `SOURCES`; `project.yml` and the `xcodegen generate` step are gone, and so is `doctor`'s xcodegen check. The scheme's test action sets `systemAttachmentLifetime = "keepNever"` and `preferredScreenCaptureFormat = "screenshots"`, which the build writes into the `.xctestrun`. A unit test fails when a source file the binary carries is not in the project.

**Why.** Every iOS user had to `brew install xcodegen` before the first command, and the GitHub Action's iOS job failed on a runner without it (`xcodegen is not installed`). Separately, Xcode records a video of every UI test session by default (`PreferredScreenCaptureFormat` was `screenRecording` in the runner's `.xctestrun`), and the runner is one test session as long as phonedriver uses the Simulator: the Simulator's testmanagerd folder on the Mac mini held 156 attachments, 154 of them QuickTime movies, up to 209 MB each, 1.2 GB in all, and the Mac encoded that video the whole time.

**Rejected.** Keeping `project.yml` beside the generated project: two sources for one thing drift. Editing the `.xctestrun` after each build: the scheme already carries both settings into it. Generating the project in Rust: a project file is data, and this one changes when a source file is added.

**Evidence.** On the Mac mini with `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` (`no xcodegen on PATH`) and a new home, the debug build built the runner (`28d4698bf27443ca`) and `doctor` showed `xcode`, `simulators`, `runner build ... up to date` and `runner ... answers` with no xcodegen line. The same runner built by `xcodebuild` from the edited scheme had `PreferredScreenCaptureFormat` `screenshots` and `SystemAttachmentLifetime` `keepNever` in its `.xctestrun`. 30 s sessions of the old and new builds, started with `xcodebuild test-without-building`: the old added a movie to the Simulator's attachments each time (`156 -> 157`, `157 -> 158`), the new none (`157 -> 157`, `158 -> 158`); a phonedriver session with the new build, `158 -> 158`. `the_project_builds_every_source_file_the_binary_carries` passes. CPU could not be compared: other sessions' processes on the mini moved more than the encoder.

**Limits.** Adding a source file to the runner now means adding it to the project in Xcode; the unit test says which file is missing. Xcode 14 and older cannot open the project, and they cannot build the runner's iOS 17 target either. Movies that earlier versions left stay in each Simulator until it is erased.

**Reopen if.** A runner change needs build settings that are easier to keep in a spec than in the project.

## D92. On an Android emulator, `shell "emu ..."` goes to the emulator's console

**Choice.** A `shell` command on an Android emulator that starts with `emu ` is sent to the emulator's console, as `adb emu` sends it, over the connection `press fingerprint` uses (D63, D68: the console port on this machine, or `nc 10.0.2.2 <port>` from inside the emulator when adb is on another machine), with the token from `~/.emulator_console_auth_token`. The reply is the shell reply: exit 0 and what the console said, or exit 1 and why it refused. On a phone the command is refused (`emu` commands go to an Android emulator's console, and a phone has none). No new verb and no new MCP tool.

**Why.** Tests of maps, delivery and ride apps need a location, and the iOS Simulator already had it through `shell "simctl location set <lat>,<lon>"` (`simctl` subcommands get the device filled in). The console also sends text messages, sets the battery and the network's speed, and rotates the sensors, all of which a test of an app wants and none of which a device shell can do.

**Rejected.** A `location` verb for both platforms: it would take the order of latitude and longitude off the user's hands (the console's `geo fix` takes longitude first), but a verb and its MCP schema cost every agent tokens on every turn, and the console has dozens of commands a verb per command would not cover.

**Evidence.** `console::tests::a_command_answers_with_what_the_console_said_or_why_it_refused` (and the four touch tests, which now go through the same `run_on`). Live, the API 37 emulator on win-pc reached from the Mac mini through `ADB_SERVER_SOCKET`, debug build: `shell "emu avd name"` answered `exit 0` and `Pixel_API_37` in 970 ms; `shell "emu geo fix -122.4194 37.7749"` `exit 0`; `shell "emu geo fixx 1"` `exit 1` with `the emulator console refused: bad sub-command`; with the mini's token file from an earlier emulator, `the emulator console refused: authentication token does not match ~/.emulator_console_auth_token`.

**Limits.** That the location reached apps was not checked: `dumpsys location` showed `last location=null` with no app asking for one. The console answers only to the token of the user who started the emulator.

**Amended 2026-11-21 by D108 (GHSA-mfp4-j74c-44xh item 1).** "Dozens of commands" included some that act on the computer that runs the emulator, not on the emulator: `screenrecord start <file>` and `screenrecord screenshot <dir>` write files there, `network capture start <file>` too, `redir add` opens a port on it, `kill` ends the emulator and `avd snapshot` changes its saved state. And a line break inside the command sent a second one. Now a command with a line break or any other control character is refused, and without `PHONEDRIVER_HOST_SHELL=1` only the commands this decision was made for are sent: `geo`, `sms`, `power`, `network speed|delay|status`, `sensor`, `rotate`, `finger` and `avd name|status`.


## D93. On the iOS Simulator, one finger's touches and the settle's frames go through simio

**Choice.** A helper, simio (`ios-runner/SimIO/simio.m`), compiled with Xcode's clang on first use and kept under `~/.phonedriver/simio/<source hash>`, holds the Simulator's own HID port and screen buffer through CoreSimulator and SimulatorKit, as idb does.
- A path with one finger in an upright app goes to `SimDeviceLegacyHIDClient` as Indigo digitizer messages. Several fingers, a turned app, a physical device and a path that starts in the top 200 points under a notification banner (the runner's new `banner` op says whether one covers the point, and a failed answer counts as covered) stay with the runner. Edge swipes go through HID too.
- The helper also prints the main display's luma each time its `IOSurface` seed changes, at most every 16 ms, and SpringBoard's lock state. The device publishes the luma into its `FrameStore` and stops polling runner screenshots for settle while it does. `Device::frames_follow` is true for it, so the settle counts from the request; the device does not claim `FRAMES`, which would make `scroll --until` fling, and the runner cannot play a fling.
- While any input is in flight the actor reads the frame channel (`Actor::watching`), and the detector gets each change at the moment it showed.
- A request that names a Simulator the last `simctl` listing holds is answered from a listing up to 30 s old while a new one is taken in the background.
- A batch's trace adds up the phases of its steps; it printed the last step's only.

If the helper cannot build or start, the device works as before: touches through the runner, settle on polled screenshots.

**Why.** On simulator-use's benchmark (its own harness, `node bench/run.mjs`, iPhone 17 Simulator, iOS 26.5, the Mac mini, release builds, a reboot before each tool), phonedriver read the screen in 117 ms, tapped in 842 ms, typed in 1,211 ms, signed in step by step in 3.63 s and in one batch in 4.35 s, slower than simulator-use on four of the five.
- A tap through the runner is 330 ms of XCTest event synthesis before the settle starts; through the HID port the testbed logs it within a few milliseconds.
- Polled screenshots cost 75 ms each and share the runner with the input, so a change was seen late.
- XCTest confirms typed text about 220 ms after the app draws it, and the frame channel keeps only the newest frame; the type's settle first read the change when the input returned and waited its quiet window again from there.
- Every other call waited 110 ms for `simctl list`, because the 2 s reuse ran out between calls.

**Rejected.**
- Typing through the HID keyboard. 16 characters went in within 10 ms, but in the testbed's default text field `x "q" 'r' -- teh ok. Gary@Mail.com` came out as `x ‘q’ ‘r’ — teh ok. gary2mail.com`: shift lost (also once unpaced in the demo app, where 15 later runs were exact) and smart punctuation applied. The runner's text input was exact.
- Declaring `FRAMES` for the Simulator: it also changes `launch`'s wait and makes scrolls fling, and a fling through the runner (a turned app) moves nothing.
- Keeping edge swipes on the runner: the rule was written for an observed failure that turned out to fail through the runner too (below).

**Evidence.**
- `a_tap_through_the_hid_port_returns_in_under_150_ms_and_lands` (`crates/ios/tests/device_live.rs`): the testbed logs `b9` and the call returns under 150 ms.
- `paste_keeps_quotes_dashes_and_capitals_in_a_field_with_smart_punctuation` passes through the runner; the same string through HID keys gave the line above.
- `text_the_screen_shows_before_the_device_confirms_it_settles_from_when_it_showed` (`--test daemon`, a fake whose text draws at 40 ms and returns at 220 ms): 370 ms before `watching`, 320 ms after. `a_batch_trace_adds_up_the_phases_of_its_steps`: the trace said `settle: 450` for a 1,400 ms batch of two settles before, at least 600 after. `a_listing_is_kept_two_seconds_and_answers_for_a_simulator_it_holds_while_a_new_one_is_taken_until_thirty`.
- Live on the mini: `device_live` 18 of 21 and `e2e_ios` 9 of 9, twice. The three that fail fail the same way without this change: two need `simctl pbcopy`, which exits 139 on that Mac even by hand, and `back_without_a_back_button_swipes_from_the_left_edge` fails at `ca9a5b3` (the 0.1.5 version commit, runner and Rust as they were) as through HID.
- The benchmark again, same setup: read 28 ms, tap 310, type 843, sign-in step by step 1.76 s, in one batch 1.75 s, at 59, 20, 13, 173 and 177 tokens. The type's remaining settle is the keyboard rising after the focus tap from a fresh launch; inside the sign-in a type settles in 90 ms.

**Limits.** SimulatorKit and CoreSimulator are private. simio was run with Xcode 27 only (it also looks for SimulatorKit where Xcode 16 kept it), and an Xcode that renames what it uses leaves the device on the runner. The helper reads the display at 0.25 pixels per point, enough for settle and nothing else; `screenshot` still asks the runner. Why the testbed's web view stopped going back from an edge swipe is open. Android was not rerun after `watching`: win-pc's adb server hung (other sessions' disk load).

## D94. The home page shows lanes and saved runs as recordings, and names `init` first

**Choice.** (1) The stop that said "Your screen stays yours" with two stills is now the lanes (`#lanes`, `site/lib/lanes-engine.ts`): one recording of two sessions on one emulator, each on its own lane, with the phone's own screen beside them. Each lane has a dotted track; a call is a bar from the moment it was sent to its reply, a ball rides the track, and the lane's screen shows the frame that display had at that moment. All lanes share one clock, played at twice the recorded speed, and a range input over the tracks scrubs it. (2) A new stop, `#saved-runs`, shows a saved run, its first replay and a later replay that stopped, with the kept screen and the found screen side by side and the changed text boxed. The GitHub Action is a short text and a workflow under it. (3) The hero, the install box, the setup prompt, three FAQ answers and `llms.txt` name `npx -y phonedriver init`; the per-client commands stay as tabs. (4) The tool list is the seventeen of 0.1.5, the speed stop adds the Simulator's read and tap from the published benchmark, and a list under "How it works" links what else is new. The page has nine stops.

**Why.** The user asked for the site to catch up with 0.1.5 and for the second-screen section to show several agents as lanes. Saved runs and the Action were the largest part of 0.1.5 and were not on the home page.

**Evidence.** `docs/bench/data/lanes-2026-11-18/`: `Pixel_API_37` on win-pc started over ssh (software rendering), phonedriver 0.1.5 on the same machine. Lane `a` (session `one`): `launch settings`, `scroll down --until "About emulated device"`, `tap "About emulated device"`, `tap "Android version"`. Lane `b` (session `two`): `launch clock`, `tap "5"`, `tap "00"`, `tap "Start"`, `tap "Add 1 minute"`. Three runs side by side took 21.1, 21.8 and 23.9 s; three with one lane after the other took 26.8, 27.4 and 26.3 s (`runs.txt`). The page plays the 21.8 s run and quotes the 26.8 s one. A loop on the device took a `screencap` of each lane's display for the whole run, about one frame a second for each lane, so the timings include that load; a frame is placed by the device's clock less the offset to the host's measured before the run. `docs/bench/data/replay-2026-11-18/`: the same emulator through the ssh tunnel, phonedriver 0.1.5 on the Mac mini. `save` wrote 3 steps, the first `replay --screens` kept 3 screens in 9.6 s, a second matched all 3, and after `svc wifi disable` the third stopped at step 2 with `~ "AndroidWifi" → "T-Mobile" at 111,158`. Wi-Fi was turned on again after.

**Limits.** Two lanes, not three: a third lane on this emulator (three software encoders) answered `device android:emulator-5554+c disconnected`, `devices` listed it with display `main`, and the guest stopped answering adb until it was restarted. Not looked into further. The side-by-side gain is small here (22 s against 27 s) because both launches wait on one software-rendered guest. On the Windows host the same `replay --screens` failed on an unchanged screen, with Windows' text recognition reading one word differently in two captures (`~ "Assistant. recent apps. default apps" → "Asststant. recent apps. default apps"`; the capture is in `replay-2026-11-18/windows-host.txt`), which is why the replay was recorded from the Mac. The figures were looked at in headless Chrome at 1440 and 390 px, dark and light, not on a real phone.

**Reopen if.** Three or more lanes are shown to work, a GPU-backed recording changes the timings, or the range input over the tracks gets in the way of scrolling on a phone.

**Amended the same day (2026-11-18), after the user saw it.** "it feels a biiit wordy still", "why not add like simulator use and argent in the visual like latency balls only why extra numbers", "the features should be displayed in easy to understand and really nice visual pleasing animation", then "it can run upto 5 lanes, show 5 lanes". What changed:

- The lanes are five agents, each a video of its own hidden screen (`phonedriver record` on each lane), played on one clock at real speed, with a dot where each tap landed (the `at` of the recording's steps file). The cards are dealt from the phone's own screen when the stop lands; pointing at one dims the rest; one slider scrubs all five. The bars, the devices table and the note are gone.
- The saved run is one phone and one list in three recordings: an agent does it and each action lands in the list, the list replays it, and a replay after Wi-Fi was turned off stops at step 4 of 5 with the changed text boxed and the chauffeur's palm raised (`mountHold`). Three buttons choose the recording and a row jumps to its step. The terminal output is behind "Show the output".
- The race has a device switch (Android emulator, real phone, iOS Simulator). The Simulator race has all four tools of the published benchmark and says where phonedriver placed in each call, second in two of five. The two rows of figures under the race are gone.
- Each stop's introduction is one sentence, and what else is new is a row of chips.

**Evidence for the amendment.** The emulator was started in win-pc's desktop session with `-gpu host` (the `pdemu` task). Five lanes opened in 1.9 to 2.6 s each one after another; Calendar on a fifth lane landed on a Google sign-in screen, so the fifth app is Phone. The recorded run: `launch` plus one to four taps on each of Settings, Clock, Contacts, Files and Phone, all five at once, 10.9 s, every call exit 0 (`docs/bench/data/lanes-2026-11-18/`). The saved run: `saved 5 steps`, `replayed 5 steps in 6.3 s` keeping 5 screens, `5 screens matched`, then `error: step 4 of 5 (tap "Network & internet") failed` with `~ "AndroidWifi" → "T-Mobile" at 111,158` (`docs/bench/data/replay-2026-11-18/`). The earlier limit of two lanes was the software-rendered emulator, not phonedriver.

**Limits of the amendment.** One run of five lanes, not repeated. The videos are 320 px wide at 20 frames a second. Contacts shows the benchmark's made-up contacts. Looked at in headless Chrome at 1440 and 390 px; video autoplay on a real iPhone was not tried. The palm is not drawn below 900 px.

**Amended again (2026-11-18), after the user saw the second version.** On the Simulator race: "some feel too slow for like for a user", "1st 2nd dont need to be shown", "the type into field and sign in one batch should be put at the last end", "a small note should be added"; and for the race and the saved run, "innovative, creative, visual way to show". What changed:

- The race is a beat. Every lane of a call runs for as long as the slowest tool takes to finish that call once, and stamps a dot each time its own call finishes, so a faster tool draws a denser line and the slowest lands one dot at the end. All calls share one sweep of 2.6 s, so nothing waits on a 5 s call. The speed switch is gone.
- The Simulator race has no places. Its order is read, tap, sign-in step by step, type, one batch, and it ends with a note on why simulator-use types faster, from `docs/bench/simulator.md`: it presses keys on the Simulator's hardware keyboard, so the on-screen keyboard never opens, and phonedriver types as it does on an iPhone. Every number is still shown.
- The saved run is a tape beside the phone. While the agent drives, each action travels as a dotted line from where it landed on the phone to the tape and becomes a stop. On replay the tape rewinds and feeds the stops through a gate, and each travels to the phone. After the change the tape jams at stop 4.

**Limits.** The lanes of different calls are on different time scales (each its own slowest tool), which the caption says. The dotted line to the phone is drawn, not recorded: its ends are the recorded tap position and the step's time.

**The beat was reverted the same day.** The user: "this feels so bad, like this isnt how latency balls work.. pls revert this part." The race is balls out and back again, with the speed switch. The Simulator order, the removed places and the note stay.

## D95: figures sit on the page, joined by the route (2026-11-18)

**Decision.** The lanes, the saved run, the approval and "how it works" are redrawn after six pictures the user sent ("take this visual style and make the website feel better"; of the approval one, "this is so much better"). A figure is no longer a dark pane with a caption bar. It is recordings placed on the page, the dotted orange route joining them, and a still field of glyphs behind. The pane stays for what a terminal printed.

- Lanes: the phone's own screen on the left, five recordings rising as a staircase, a route from one point on the phone to the foot of each.
- Saved run: a route of five numbered stops that a marker drives; after the change it halts at 04 and the screen saved with that step (`replay --screens` kept it) comes up beside the one on the phone, both with the changed text boxed. This replaces the tape of the same morning.
- Approval: the heading, one sentence, the question as it was asked, the Buy button cropped from the recording's poster with "held" on it, the palm, and the closing line. The three facts became one sentence and a link.
- How it works: one recorded tap (`tap "Display & touch"`, the second step of the saved run) traced from the agent through the daemon to the phone, with the first five lines of its real reply.

**Why.** The user's pictures, and their earlier notes that the stops were wordy and the figures should be visual.

**Evidence.** Mac mini: `pnpm typecheck` clean, `pnpm build` clean, `node scripts/check.mjs` ends `problems 0`. Looked at in headless Chrome: lanes at 1440 px dark and 390 px light; the saved run in all three recordings at 1440 px (dark and light) and at 390 px; the approval at 1440 and 390 px; the trace mid-flight and finished at 1440 px and finished at 390 px.

**Limits.** The route lines, the marker and the travelling dot are drawn; their ends and times come from the recordings. The trace's legs are not to scale: the whole tap took under two seconds and the dot's crossing is slowed to be seen. The pictures the user sent also showed a ribbon of overlapping screens and a large 0 for the saved run; those were not built. Not looked at on a real phone.

**D95, one pass for coherence (2026-11-18).** The user asked whether the whole site could be made more coherent. Three things were out of step and are changed: the race was the last figure still in a dark pane and now sits on the page like the others (the pane is left for the snapshot, the question and the setup prompt, which are text a terminal showed); its device switch uses the same underlined tabs as the saved run and the setup prompt; and the saved run, which looped with no way to stop it, has the same Play and Pause control as the lanes. The five limits are questions in the list, closed by default. Not changed: the first two stops and the last, which already followed the contract.


## D96. Off a Mac, phonedriver mounts the disk image itself and starts a runner the person installed

**Choice.** On Windows and Linux (`setup::XCODE` is false) a physical iPhone or iPad on iOS 17 to 26 is driven by the runner tier without Xcode:

- The runner is built for devices, unsigned, by the `runner-ipa` job of the release workflow on GitHub's macOS machine (`xcodebuild build-for-testing ... CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`), packed as `PhonedriverRunner.ipa` without the runner app's `Frameworks` folder (XCTest then loads from the device's developer disk image, as Appium's unsigned WebDriverAgent does), and attached to each `v*` release, so each version downloads the runner built from its own commit (`runner::RUNNER_IPA` names this version's). A rolling release built from `main` was rejected: a released CLI would download a runner from later sources.
- The person signs and installs it with Sideloadly and their own Apple ID. phonedriver finds it with the installation proxy by its executable, `PhonedriverRunner-Runner`, because a signing tool may change the bundle id.
- The daemon starts it with idevice's `XCUITestService` (testmanagerd through the CoreDevice tunnel on iOS 17+), passes `PHONEDRIVER_PORT`, `PHONEDRIVER_BUILD` and `PHONEDRIVER_UDID` in the runner's environment, and reaches it at device port 27753 through usbmuxd as on a Mac. A runner whose `hello` reports another wire version is refused with the reason.
