Honouring `device` per step: a batch is one lease, one screen and one diff. A timeout on every kind of step: only `wait` takes one as a tool, and the batch's covers each action's settle.

**Cost.** No schema text. An older daemon ignores a wait step's `timeout_ms` and uses the batch's, as before.

## D112. `launch` with `restart` clears the app's task on Android (2026-11-24, #51)

**Choice.** `Device::launch` takes `fresh`. `launch` with `restart` still stops the app (`am force-stop`) and then starts its launcher activity with `FLAG_ACTIVITY_CLEAR_TASK` beside `FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK` (agent op `launch`, `fresh: true`). A plain `launch` is unchanged and brings the app back where it was. iOS ignores `fresh`: a terminated app already starts on its first screen.

**Why.** Issue #51, API 37 emulator: from Settings, the search screen (`com.google.android.settings.intelligence`) and Default apps (`com.google.android.permissioncontroller`) are activities of another package inside the Settings task. A force-stop of Settings ends Settings' own activities and leaves that one, and a plain start brings the task forward with it on top, so every `launch Settings --restart` answered "Settings is not in front" and a saved run that starts with `launch restart` could not start clean. Stopping the other package by hand was the only way out.

**Evidence.** Mac mini to the win-pc emulator (`Pixel_API_37`, animations on), 2026-11-24. With the 0.1.4 binary: `restarted Settings, but Settings is not in front (com.google.android.settings.intelligence is)`, twice. With this change: 8 of 8 restarts from the two pages opened on Settings' first screen (two runs, four from each page), and `launch Settings` after `press home` from the Apps page came back to the Apps page.

**Rejected.** Stopping whatever package is in front as well: it may be a system component other apps share (the permission controller). Clearing the task on every launch: `launch` promises the app where it was left.

**Limits.** An app whose launcher activity only forwards to another task is not helped by clearing its own.


## D113. `check` reads one value, alone or as a batch step (2026-11-24, #52)

**Choice.** A daemon method `check {target, equals?, as?}` and a `Step` of that kind. It resolves the target as an action does, reads a value for it and touches nothing. The value is the first of: `on` or `off` for a checkable element; the element's value; the second text its snapshot line shows (a row's summary), except a text field's placeholder; for a text target, the rest of a label that holds the text and more; the element on the same row to its right, else the non-actionable text right under it within one label-height. With none of those the reading is `"X" is shown`. A text that names elements inside one another (a row and its switch) reads the checkable one, else the outermost. `equals` compares after `text::fold` and with runs of spaces as one, and a mismatch is `NotAsExpected` (-32027), which ends a batch like any failed step. A password field is `Refused`. The CLI verb is `check`; over MCP it is a `batch` step only.

**Why.** Issue #52's first proposal. A flow that taps Pay after reading a total needed three model turns: a batch up to the total, a read, a batch after it. With a check step it is one call, and with `equals` the batch stops before the tap when the total is not the one the model planned for. `wait "€24.99"` proves the text is somewhere on the screen, not that it is the total. A saved run had the same gap: it could wait for a value and never say which label it belonged to.

**Not an MCP tool.** The tool list is read every turn (budget test, 6,700 characters). A single check over MCP is a batch of one step; the batch description carries it in one clause.

**Secrets and untrusted text.** The reply and the error leave through `Daemon::call`, so a remembered secret the field shows is masked (D70); the secrets property test generates checks of both fields, right and wrong, and break B1 shows a check leaking in the reply and in the trace when the mask is removed. The default trace keeps `checked` or the error's name and the length of `equals`, never the value. A value that `inject::looks_like_instruction` flags is marked `(ai-instruction)` like a snapshot line. An app scope applies as to any read: the target resolves on the scoped screen.

**Evidence.** Mac mini to the win-pc emulator (`Pixel_API_37`), Settings, 2026-11-24: `check "Network & internet"` answered `"Network & internet" = "Mobile, Wi‑Fi, hotspot"`; `check "Dark theme"` on the Display page answered `"Dark theme" = "off"`; `check "Brightness level" --equals "0%"` failed with `shows "83%", not "0%"`; a batch stopped at its third step on a wrong `equals` with the two steps before it listed; a saved run of seven steps with four checks replayed 7 of 7 in 4.7 s. The first live run is why the row summary and the row-and-switch rule exist: the fake phone's rows had neither, `check "Network & internet"` said only `is shown`, and `check "Dark theme"` was refused as ambiguous.

**Rejected.** Reading several values in one step (several steps do it, each named with `as`); a pattern or a numeric comparison in `equals` (no flow asked for one); returning values as structured fields beside the text (the numbered line is what the model reads, and a client can parse it).

**Limits.** "Beside" is geometry within one parent: a value in another container, or a table whose cells share no parent with their header, reads `is shown`. OCR-only screens read what OCR found. iOS was checked on the fake only.

## D114. An action that finds no element answers with the screen (2026-11-24, #37)

**Choice.** A failed `act` or `check` whose error is `NotFound`, or `StaleRef` for a ref the session did see, carries `screen`: the screen as the daemon just read it, as the diff against the one the session was last shown (the whole screen when there is nothing to diff against, `""` when nothing changed). The session's last screen becomes that one, so the refs in the error are valid for the next call. The hints stop telling the agent to run `snapshot`; with `""` they say the screen is as it was last seen. The read is the one the failed resolve already made (`guard_view`), so the failure costs no extra tree read.

**Why.** Issue #37: after a tap on text that was gone, the agent's next call was always `snapshot`, a turn that carries nothing the daemon did not already hold. A failed batch already returns the screen with its failure; a single action now does the same.

**Not on every error.** `Ambiguous` already lists the candidates; `OffScreen`, `NeedsUser` and the rest name their own next step. A ref from another session gets no screen: there is no screen of that session to measure against, and the hint to read one first stays.

**Evidence.** Same rig. The screen was changed behind the session (Settings sent to the launcher with `adb shell input keyevent 4`), then `tap "Dark theme"`: the error printed `screen now, against what you last saw:` and the launcher's lines with new refs. The same tap with the screen unchanged printed the hint `The screen is as you last saw it, and nothing on it matches.` and no lines. Daemon test `a_tap_that_finds_no_element_answers_with_the_screen_as_it_is_now` asserts one tree read per failure and that a ref taken from the error taps.

## D115. Small MCP gaps closed; directions are a fixed list, keys are not (2026-11-24, #52)

**Choice.** `logs` takes `since`; `replay` takes `screens`, a folder that must be inside the server's own folder (no `..`, checked again after symlinks resolve); `snapshot` shows `audit` in its schema; the `devices` description says what `<id>+name` is; `direction` on `scroll` and `swipe` is `{"enum": ["up","down","left","right"]}`. `since` and `screens` are accepted and hidden from the lean schema, like the other arguments an agent meets only through a hint that names them.

**Why.** Each was something the CLI did that an MCP-only agent could not, or had to learn from an error. The enum costs the characters the sentence it replaces did.

**Keys stay prose.** `press` takes nineteen names and the description says what the unusual ones do (`landscape`, `fingerprint` on an emulator only). An enum would add about 200 characters to every turn and say less; a wrong key is already answered with the valid ones.

**Budget.** The tool list is 6,700 characters at most now (6,600 before): lanes in `devices` and the `check` clause in `batch` are about 160.

## D116. `scroll` with `collect` returns a list's rows across scrolls (2026-11-24, #52)

**Choice.** `Action::Scroll` takes `collect: n`. The daemon reads the list's rows, drags one scroll (the same 60 percent drag a plain `scroll` makes, never a fling), reads again, and repeats until it has `n` rows, the list ends, or `max` scrolls (30). The reply is an `ActionResult` with `rows`, the summary `collected 22 rows in 3 scrolls down: the list ended` (or `stopped at the limit of 60`, `stopped after 30 scrolls`), and the usual observation of the screen it ended on. `collect` with `until` is `Refused`, and so is a screen with nothing that scrolls that way. CLI `scroll down --collect [N]` (100 without a number); MCP `scroll` with `collect`; a batch step lists its rows under its numbered line.

**The list** is `in` when given, else the largest element that scrolls in the asked direction, else the largest that scrolls on that axis. **A row** is a direct child of the list (read through a lone wrapper that cannot be tapped): its snapshot line without the ref, then the lines of what it holds, joined with `, `.

**Lining pages up.** Consecutive pages share rows. The new page's rows are matched against the end of what is held, by text, and by position too where both rows carry one; the longest overlap is dropped and the rest appended. Up to two leading rows of a page may be skipped as cut-off copies. The last row held may itself have been cut off by the page's edge: when only the rows before it line up, the page's own row replaces it. Scrolling up or left is the same with both lists reversed.

**The end** is a list that no longer has the scroll action for that direction, or two drags in a row that add no row (for lists that always report both directions).

**Why.** Issue #52, measured there on `main`: two single scrolls of All apps showed 17 new rows, a batch of the same two showed 11, because a batch answers with one diff (D17). Reading a long list took a turn per page.

**Evidence.** Mac mini to the win-pc emulator (`Pixel_API_37`), Settings, 2026-11-24. The first run returned 34 rows for a 22-row list: `button "Storage"` at the foot of page one was `button "Storage" "51% used - 7.78 GB free"` on page two, so the overlap never matched, and a row cut at the top came back as a bare `button`. With the cut-row rule: 22 rows, once each, in 3 scrolls and 3.8 s, and the same 22 with `scroll up --collect 40` from the bottom. On the Display page the first run returned two rows, the page's header and the whole list as one line: the page is a group that scrolls up only around the list that scrolls down, and the group is larger. With the direction rule: `collected 17 rows in 1 scroll down: the list ended` in 1.2 s, where the snapshot says `list 17 rows`.

**Rejected.** Named fields per row (`row`, `fields`), which both reviewers in #52 wanted: a row's line already separates label, value and state, and a schema for fields would cost every turn. Returning refs for rows: a ref for a row that scrolled off is stale by the time the reply is read; the final screen carries the live ones. A fling to go faster: rows pass unread.

**Limits.** Two rows with the same text and no positions, where one page ends and the next begins, count once. A row whose text changes while the list scrolls (a clock, a progress line) is listed twice. A list that loads more as it is scrolled is collected as far as it had loaded when the drags stopped adding rows. Checked on Android; on iOS on the fake only.

## D117. Notifications are listed and waited for from the platform's list, not the shade (2026-11-24, #52)

**Choice.** `Device::notifications()` returns `Notices {now_ms, notices}`, newest first, each with package, title, text, post time on the device's clock, and whether it is ongoing. Android runs `date +%s; dumpsys notification --noredact` in the agent's shell and parses the `Notification List` section (`crates/android/src/notifications.rs`); a group summary with no text of its own is dropped. Every other device answers `Unsupported`. The daemon method `notifications {app?, limit?}` renders a line each. `WaitCondition::Notification {pattern, app?}` polls once a second for a notification posted since the session's last action began (at most 120 s back; since the wait began when the session has done none) whose title or text holds one of the words. Its reply's summary names the app and `rows` holds the line. CLI `notifications` and `wait notification:"text"`; MCP `snapshot` with `notifications` and `wait` with `notification`.

**Why.** Issue #52, measured there on 0.1.7: reading a notification took `press notifications` (2.2 s, 1,108 characters of quick settings and every other notification) and `press back` (1.6 s), and a group came back collapsed. `cmd notification list` prints keys with no text.

**Since the last action.** The flow is tap `Send code`, then wait. A code from an hour ago that is still up must not be the answer, and the new one may arrive before the wait is called. So the window opens when the session's last action began, measured on the daemon's clock and applied to the device's.

**Why the dump and not the agent.** `INotificationManager.getActiveNotifications` needs `ACCESS_NOTIFICATIONS`, which the shell user does not hold on API 37 (it holds `MANAGE_NOTIFICATIONS` and `MANAGE_NOTIFICATION_LISTENERS`). `cmd notification get <key>` prints the same record one at a time. The dump is one call.

**Untrusted text, secrets, scope.** A line whose title or text `inject::looks_like_instruction` flags ends in `(ai-instruction)`; line breaks in a text and control characters in an app's name cannot start a line. The texts are in the reply only: the trace keeps the method name or `a notification from <app> is up` and the length of the pattern, and a saved step keeps `a notification matched`. Replies leave through `Daemon::call`, so remembered secrets are masked (D70); the secrets property test lists and waits on a notification that repeats every earlier secret. Under an app scope the scope wrapper drops every notification of a package outside it before the daemon sees it. This is not automatic reading of one-time codes, which #52 rules out: nothing is read unless the agent asks, and the agent still has to type what it read with `secret`.

**Evidence.** Same rig. `notifications` listed seven, among them a two-line text as `"first line\nsecond line"`, a text with parentheses, and the ongoing system one. A notification posted 4 s into a 20 s wait: `ok a notification from com.android.shell is up` and its line, 2.7 s after the wait was called. With nothing new: `timed out after 3204 ms waiting for a new notification with "fresh"; 7 are up, none new that matched`. The parser's test runs on that emulator's dump (`tests/fixtures/dumpsys_notification_api37.txt`).

**Limits.** The dump's format is Android's own and was read on API 37 only; an older or a vendor's dump that names fields differently lists fewer notifications or none, and the reply cannot tell that from an empty shade. The dump is about 215 KB, read once a second during a wait. Messaging-style notifications give their last message, not the thread. A notification an app posts with a time in the past (a message's own time) can fall before the window. Toasts are not notifications (D23). iOS: not supported.
